People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. It… it kind of started getting really steady the following summer. Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.

But since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.

It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.

Everything’s kind of slipping away, and I’m just… letting it.\n\nDoctor: How are you feeling most of the time nowadays, are you feeling sad or empty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… empty, mostly. Sad too, but it’s more like this heavy hollow feeling, right in my chest, when I wake up most days.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll look at the wall in my apartment, and there’s this little crack near the window I keep meaning to fix, or maybe it’s just paint, I don’t know… and I just feel like that’s all there is. Like there’s no future past those walls.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this empty and hollow?\n\nPatient: It… it kind of started getting really steady the following summer. Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.\n\nBut since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.

And she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.

So… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.

coffee feels like lifting wet cement.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after I’ve been And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\n[Last Question]\nIs the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.

It’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.3, F31.3, F20.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I just don’t care about anything anymore. Like, I used to go to my art classes, and there were board-game nights, and hiking—there’s this one trail I always meant to finish, it has these weird little signs about birds—but now I just sit in my room.\n\nEven soccer, I kept saying I’d get back to the team, and I don’t even feel anything about it now. Everything’s kind of slipping away, and I’m just… letting it.\n\nDoctor: How are you feeling most of the time nowadays, are you feeling sad or empty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… empty, mostly. Sad too, but it’s more like this heavy hollow feeling, right in my chest, when I wake up most days.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll look at the wall in my apartment, and there’s this little crack near the window I keep meaning to fix, or maybe it’s just paint, I don’t know… and I just feel like that’s all there is. Like there’s no future past those walls.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this empty and hollow?\n\nPatient: It… it kind of started getting really steady the following summer. Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.\n\nBut since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.

Energy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.

So… I’m not sure. What do you mean by “a lot of energy”?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I just don’t care about anything anymore. Like, I used to go to my art classes, and there were board-game nights, and hiking—there’s this one trail I always meant to finish, it has these weird little signs about birds—but now I just sit in my room.\n\nEven soccer, I kept saying I’d get back to the team, and I don’t even feel anything about it now. Everything’s kind of slipping away, and I’m just… letting it.\n\nDoctor: How are you feeling most of the time nowadays, are you feeling sad or empty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… empty, mostly. Sad too, but it’s more like this heavy hollow feeling, right in my chest, when I wake up most days.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll look at the wall in my apartment, and there’s this little crack near the window I keep meaning to fix, or maybe it’s just paint, I don’t know… and I just feel like that’s all there is. Like there’s no future past those walls.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this empty and hollow?\n\nPatient: It… it kind of started getting really steady the following summer. Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.\n\nBut since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know. I keep getting stuck on what “a lot of energy” means, because sometimes I’m restless, but it’s not like I feel good.

It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. What do you mean by “a lot of energy”?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods or the woman's voice happen at specific times of the day, week, or month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t noticed a real pattern like that. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think, “Oh, maybe it’s worse when the room is quiet,” but then I’ll have a quiet day and nothing really happens, so I don’t know if that means anything.\n\nI’ve thought about writing it down, like in one of those little notebooks—I have one somewhere with old grocery lists in it, I think—but then I forget, or I look back and it doesn’t make sense. So I can’t really say it’s a certain day or time of the month. It just feels like it happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Several days… I don’t know. I keep getting stuck on what “a lot of energy” means, because sometimes I’m restless, but it’s not like I feel good.\n\nI used to drink too much coffee when I was in school—well, I wasn’t really sleeping right then either, but that was a long time ago, and I don’t know if that counts. My aunt used to say I was “wired,” but she says that about everyone if they talk too fast.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I can’t really point to a stretch of days and say, yes, that was definitely that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. What do you mean by “a lot of energy”?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods or the woman's voice happen at specific times of the day, week, or month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t noticed a real pattern like that. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think, “Oh, maybe it’s worse when the room is quiet,” but then I’ll have a quiet day and nothing really happens, so I don’t know if that means anything.\n\nI’ve thought about writing it down, like in one of those little notebooks—I have one somewhere with old grocery lists in it, I think—but then I forget, or I look back and it doesn’t make sense. It just feels like it happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Several days… I don’t know. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A whole week… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of it like seven days in a row, because weeks get blurry for me—like Monday and Thursday feel kind of the same if I’m not doing anything scheduled.

I’ve had times I was irritable and couldn’t really sit still, but I can’t say it lasted a week, not clearly. And “extremely happy” doesn’t really fit. I mean, sometimes I’ll get caught up doing something, like reorganizing a drawer, and then I find old receipts and start thinking about why I kept them, and then suddenly I’ve wasted the afternoon… but that’s not like having amazing energy for a week.

So I guess I’m not sure. I can’t point to a full week like that.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F31.3, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I just don’t care about anything anymore. Like, I used to go to my art classes, and there were board-game nights, and hiking—there’s this one trail I always meant to finish, it has these weird little signs about birds—but now I just sit in my room.\n\nEven soccer, I kept saying I’d get back to the team, and I don’t even feel anything about it now. Everything’s kind of slipping away, and I’m just… letting it.\n\nDoctor: How are you feeling most of the time nowadays, are you feeling sad or empty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… empty, mostly. Sad too, but it’s more like this heavy hollow feeling, right in my chest, when I wake up most days.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll look at the wall in my apartment, and there’s this little crack near the window I keep meaning to fix, or maybe it’s just paint, I don’t know… and I just feel like that’s all there is. Like there’s no future past those walls.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this empty and hollow?\n\nPatient: It… it kind of started getting really steady the following summer. Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.\n\nBut since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. What do you mean by “a lot of energy”?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods or the woman's voice happen at specific times of the day, week, or month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t noticed a real pattern like that. I don’t keep track very well. Like, I used to go to my art classes, and there were board-game nights, and hiking—there’s this one trail I always meant to finish, it has these weird little signs about birds—but now I just sit in my room.\n\nEven soccer, I kept saying I’d get back to the team, and I don’t even feel anything about it now. Everything’s kind of slipping away, and I’m just… letting it.\n\nDoctor: How are you feeling most of the time nowadays, are you feeling sad or empty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… empty, mostly. Sad too, but it’s more like this heavy hollow feeling, right in my chest, when I wake up most days.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll look at the wall in my apartment, and there’s this little crack near the window I keep meaning to fix, or maybe it’s just paint, I don’t know… and I just feel like that’s all there is. Like there’s no future past those walls.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this empty and hollow?\n\nPatient: It… it kind of started getting really steady the following summer. Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.\n\nBut since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. So I can’t really say it’s a certain day or time of the month. It just feels like it happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Several days… I don’t know. I keep getting stuck on what “a lot of energy” means, because sometimes I’m restless, but it’s not like I feel good.\n\nI used to drink too much coffee when I was in school—well, I wasn’t really sleeping right then either, but that was a long time ago, and I don’t know if that counts. I can’t really point to a stretch of days and say, yes, that was definitely that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for at least one week?\n\nPatient: A whole week… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of it like seven days in a row, because weeks get blurry for me—like Monday and Thursday feel kind of the same if I’m not doing anything scheduled.\n\nI’ve had times I was irritable and couldn’t really sit still, but I can’t say it lasted a week, not clearly. I can’t point to a full week like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I mean, several days in a row is hard for me to pin down. I don’t know about “without a clear reason.” That part is hard because I can usually find some reason after the fact, even if it’s not a good one—like the neighbors being loud, or the sink making that dripping sound, or someone using the wrong tone with me.

But several days in a row… I’m not sure. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think I’ve been in a mood for days, and then I realize it was just because I didn’t leave the apartment much and everything kind of blended together. So I can’t really say yes to that clearly.

Before that I think it came and went, or I told myself it did.\n\nBut since then it’s been there pretty much all the time. So… about a year and four months now, I guess. It’s strange, because I remember that summer mostly by how hot my apartment got and how the blinds stuck crooked, and I kept thinking I’d feel better when the weather changed, but I didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has that changed too?\n\nPatient: It’s changed a lot. I don’t really enjoy any of it now… art class, board-game nights, hiking, even soccer. I used to think about getting back to the team, and now it’s like the thought just slides off me.\n\nMostly I just sit in my room. There’s this pile of stuff by the door—shoes, an old bag, some sketch paper—and I keep telling myself I’ll move it or use it, but I don’t. Everything I used to care about feels far away, like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I keep trying to think of one, but no. I mean, I used to like art class, and board-game nights, hiking, soccer… I’d make plans in my head about going back to the team.\n\nNow I just kind of sit in my room. Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. It all just slips away before I can even care about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like— I don’t know how to explain it right. I keep having these thoughts that I just don’t want to wake up anymore. Like if I could just not have to open my eyes and do another day, that would be… easier.\n\nAnd my friend, when she found me, she said I was saying it out loud. I don’t even remember all of it clearly, but I was saying I couldn’t keep living like this. She kept asking me to sit down, and there was this mug on the floor, I remember that more than what I said… but yes. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. What do you mean by “a lot of energy”?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods or the woman's voice happen at specific times of the day, week, or month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t noticed a real pattern like that. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think, “Oh, maybe it’s worse when the room is quiet,” but then I’ll have a quiet day and nothing really happens, so I don’t know if that means anything.\n\nI’ve thought about writing it down, like in one of those little notebooks—I have one somewhere with old grocery lists in it, I think—but then I forget, or I look back and it doesn’t make sense. So I can’t really say it’s a certain day or time of the month. It just feels like it happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Several days… I don’t know. I keep getting stuck on what “a lot of energy” means, because sometimes I’m restless, but it’s not like I feel good.\n\nI used to drink too much coffee when I was in school—well, I wasn’t really sleeping right then either, but that was a long time ago, and I don’t know if that counts. My aunt used to say I was “wired,” but she says that about everyone if they talk too fast.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I can’t really point to a stretch of days and say, yes, that was definitely that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for at least one week?\n\nPatient: A whole week… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of it like seven days in a row, because weeks get blurry for me—like Monday and Thursday feel kind of the same if I’m not doing anything scheduled.\n\nI’ve had times I was irritable and couldn’t really sit still, but I can’t say it lasted a week, not clearly. And “extremely happy” doesn’t really fit. I mean, sometimes I’ll get caught up doing something, like reorganizing a drawer, and then I find old receipts and start thinking about why I kept them, and then suddenly I’ve wasted the afternoon… but that’s not like having amazing energy for a week.\n\nSo I guess I’m not sure. I can’t point to a full week like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I mean, several days in a row is hard for me to pin down. I’ve had times where I’m irritated, like everything feels too loud or people are asking too many questions, but then I can’t tell if that was a whole stretch of days or just one bad day that I remember too strongly.\n\nExtremely happy doesn’t sound right, though. I keep thinking of times where I got into something—like cleaning a shelf or sorting papers—and then I’d forget to eat lunch, but that’s not the same thing, is it? I don’t know. I can’t say for sure that I’ve had several days like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or irritable for several days in a row, without any clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “without a clear reason.” That part is hard because I can usually find some reason after the fact, even if it’s not a good one—like the neighbors being loud, or the sink making that dripping sound, or someone using the wrong tone with me.\n\nBut several days in a row… I’m not sure. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think I’ve been in a mood for days, and then I realize it was just because I didn’t leave the apartment much and everything kind of blended together. So I can’t really say yes to that clearly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Sometimes I stare at my sketch paper like I should want to use it, but it’s like there’s nothing there. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. I have thought that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, worried… I mean, I get stuck in my head a lot, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean.\n\nSometimes people ask me that and I start thinking about bills or appointments or whether I left something out, and then I’m thinking about this drawer in my room that won’t close because there’s papers in it from school—well, not school now, but when I was taking classes—and then I forget what I was even trying to answer.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it clearly like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself, but my jeans are loose now—like loose enough I have to tie or knot the waistband, which is weird because they used to fit.\n\nI’m not eating much. I’ll go most of the day and then realize I haven’t had anything, but food just tastes… flat. Like cardboard. Even things I used to like, I’ll look at them and then I just put them back. It’s kind of stupid, because there’s food there, and then I’ll get distracted by the dishes or the cabinet being open and… yeah. I guess my weight has changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than you used to, or that your thoughts are racing or hard to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… both, I think. Some days I can’t sit still, like I just keep walking from the kitchen to the window and back again, over and over, and then my legs hurt but I’m still doing it.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. Like making coffee feels… ridiculous, like everything is too heavy. Even lifting the mug or reaching for the filter feels like trying to move wet cement. I’ll just stand there for a long time and not get anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, because it sounds… I don’t know.\n\nThere’s this woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. Like near where the wall meets the dresser—well, the dresser is kind of crooked because one leg is messed up, and I keep meaning to put cardboard under it, but then I forget.\n\nBut the voice… it says I ruined everything. And that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought exactly. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed saying it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear the woman's voice in your room?\n\nPatient: It’s… not constant. It’s mostly in my room, from that corner by the dresser, and when it happens it’s really clear—like she’s standing right next to the bed. She says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside.\n\nI don’t know exactly how often… sometimes it feels like it’s been happening a lot, but then I lose track, because the days kind of blur together. And the dresser corner bothers me too, the way the shadow sits there, so I’ll stare at it and then suddenly I’m listening for her again.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real or just a product of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part that messes me up. Part of me knows maybe it’s my mind doing it, but when it happens it doesn’t feel like that. It sounds real. Like an actual woman standing right by my bed, coming from that corner of the room.\n\nAnd she says I ruined everything, that I should stay inside. I can still hear the way she says it sometimes, even when I’m not in there. Well—not hear it exactly, but remember it, like when a song gets stuck in your head except it’s not a song. I had this lamp there before, by the dresser, and the shade was torn on one side, and I kept thinking if I moved it maybe the corner would feel different… but the voice still sounded like it was from there.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s “real,” but it feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the woman's voice something you hear every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, I don’t think… but I lose track. Some days it feels like it might as well be every day, because I’m waiting for it.\n\nIt’s that same woman’s voice from the corner of my room, by the dresser, saying I ruined everything and I should stay inside. And when it happens, it’s not fuzzy or like I’m imagining it—it’s clear, like she’s right next to my bed. I’ve tried not looking at that corner, but then I end up noticing the dresser leg again, and the shadow there, and I start listening for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “extremely happy” doesn’t sound like me. Irritable, maybe, but I don’t know if it was like what you mean.\n\nEnergy is hard to say. Sometimes I’ll pace around or start cleaning one little thing and then get stuck on something else, like sorting old papers, but that doesn’t feel happy exactly. It’s more like I can’t settle. But then I also lose track of time, and I’ll think I was doing something for ten minutes and it’s been an hour.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. What do you mean by “a lot of energy”?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods or the woman's voice happen at specific times of the day, week, or month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t noticed a real pattern like that. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think, “Oh, maybe it’s worse when the room is quiet,” but then I’ll have a quiet day and nothing really happens, so I don’t know if that means anything.\n\nI’ve thought about writing it down, like in one of those little notebooks—I have one somewhere with old grocery lists in it, I think—but then I forget, or I look back and it doesn’t make sense. So I can’t really say it’s a certain day or time of the month. It just feels like it happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Several days… I don’t know. I keep getting stuck on what “a lot of energy” means, because sometimes I’m restless, but it’s not like I feel good.\n\nI used to drink too much coffee when I was in school—well, I wasn’t really sleeping right then either, but that was a long time ago, and I don’t know if that counts. My aunt used to say I was “wired,” but she says that about everyone if they talk too fast.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I can’t really point to a stretch of days and say, yes, that was definitely that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for at least one week?\n\nPatient: A whole week… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of it like seven days in a row, because weeks get blurry for me—like Monday and Thursday feel kind of the same if I’m not doing anything scheduled.\n\nI’ve had times I was irritable and couldn’t really sit still, but I can’t say it lasted a week, not clearly. And “extremely happy” doesn’t really fit. I mean, sometimes I’ll get caught up doing something, like reorganizing a drawer, and then I find old receipts and start thinking about why I kept them, and then suddenly I’ve wasted the afternoon… but that’s not like having amazing energy for a week.\n\nSo I guess I’m not sure. I can’t point to a full week like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I mean, several days in a row is hard for me to pin down. I’ve had times where I’m irritated, like everything feels too loud or people are asking too many questions, but then I can’t tell if that was a whole stretch of days or just one bad day that I remember too strongly.\n\nExtremely happy doesn’t sound right, though. I keep thinking of times where I got into something—like cleaning a shelf or sorting papers—and then I’d forget to eat lunch, but that’s not the same thing, is it? I don’t know. I can’t say for sure that I’ve had several days like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or irritable for several days in a row, without any clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “without a clear reason.” That part is hard because I can usually find some reason after the fact, even if it’s not a good one—like the neighbors being loud, or the sink making that dripping sound, or someone using the wrong tone with me.\n\nBut several days in a row… I’m not sure. I don’t keep track very well. Sometimes I think I’ve been in a mood for days, and then I realize it was just because I didn’t leave the apartment much and everything kind of blended together. So I can’t really say yes to that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean… I think I answered that weird before, sorry. I don’t know how to separate “extremely” from just being really annoyed. Like if the hallway smells like someone’s cooking onions, and then the TV is too loud, and then I start thinking about the laundry machine being broken last month—then I feel like I’ve been irritated forever, but it might just be that day.\n\nHappy, not really. Not like extremely happy for days. Irritable maybe, but I can’t tell you it was several days in a row in a clean way. I don’t track it like that."
  }
